In [10, (3.22)], without yet calling it as such, the above was expressed as the following \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(1,\alpha )$$\end{document}(1,α)-Pisier norm,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} H^\uparrow _\alpha (X|Y)_\rho = \frac{\alpha }{1-\alpha }\log \Vert \rho _{YX}\Vert _{(Y:1,X:\alpha )}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hα↑(X|Y)ρ=α1-αlog‖ρYX‖(Y:1,X:α).Similarly we have that, given a quantum channel (a CPTP map) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi :Q\rightarrow RS$$\end{document}Φ:Q→RS, the minimum \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\rightarrow (1,p)$$\end{document}1→(1,p) norm, since\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \inf _{\rho _{Q}\in \mathcal {D}(\mathcal {H}_{Q})}H_\alpha ^\uparrow (S|R)_{\Phi (\rho )}&= \inf _{\rho \in \mathcal {D}(\mathcal {H}_{Q})}\frac{\alpha }{1-\alpha }\log \Vert \Phi (\rho )\Vert _{(R:1,S:\alpha )} \\ &= \frac{\alpha }{1-\alpha }\log \sup _{\rho \ge 0} \frac{\Vert \Phi (\rho )\Vert _{(R:1,S:\alpha )}}{\Vert \rho \Vert _{(Q:1)}} \\ &= \frac{\alpha }{1-\alpha }\log \Vert \Phi \Vert ^+_{(Q:1)\rightarrow (R:1,S:\alpha )}. \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$cb,1\rightarrow (1,p)$$\end{document}cb,1→(1,p) norm we have a similar connection. Namely, its logarithm is related to the minimal conditional Rényi entropy under purifications, i.e.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\inf _{E}\inf _{\rho _{EQ}}H^\uparrow _\alpha (S|RE)_{{{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_E\otimes \Phi (\rho )} \\&\quad = \inf _{\rho _{Q}}H^\uparrow _\alpha (S|R\tilde{Q})_{({{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_{\tilde{Q}}\otimes \Phi )(|\sqrt{\rho }\rangle \langle \sqrt{\rho }|_{\tilde{Q}Q})} = \frac{\alpha }{1-\alpha }\log \Vert \Phi \Vert ^+_{cb,(Q:1)\rightarrow (R:1,S:\alpha )}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}infEinfρEQHα↑(S|RE)idE⊗Φ(ρ)=infρQHα↑(S|RQ~)(idQ~⊗Φ)(|ρ⟩⟨ρ|Q~Q)=α1-αlog‖Φ‖cb,(Q:1)→(R:1,S:α)+,where the infimum in both cases is over positive normalized states \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho $$\end{document}ρ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Q}$$\end{document}Q~ are systems isomorphic to Q. Here \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\sqrt{\rho }\rangle \langle \sqrt{\rho }|_{\tilde{Q}Q}$$\end{document}|ρ⟩⟨ρ|Q~Q is a purification of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _Q$$\end{document}ρQ with purifying system \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{Q}$$\end{document}Q~, e.g. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\sqrt{\rho }\rangle :=\sum _{i}|i\rangle _{\tilde{Q}}\otimes \sqrt{\rho _Q}|i\rangle _{Q}$$\end{document}|ρ⟩:=∑i|i⟩Q~⊗ρQ|i⟩Q. This follows from a standard Schmidt decomposition argument that yields that the CB norm is achieved on environments E which are isomorphic to the input system Q of the channel, see Lemma A.1 for details.

In the next section, we derive useful variational expressions for the norms on the operator spaces \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H}_Q,\mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H}_P,\mathcal {S}_r(\mathcal {H}_R)]]$$\end{document}Sq[HQ,Sp[HP,Sr(HR)]], i.e. for operator-valued Schatten norms of 3 indexes, departing from Theorem 2.3. These will be central to show chain rules and sub-multiplicativity of completely bounded \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\rightarrow (1,p)$$\end{document}1→(1,p) norms that will translate directly into additivity statements for conditional entropies under tensor products of quantum channels.

Generalized Variational Expressions for Pisier Norms

In this section, we first derive generalized variational formulas for relating the norms of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]$$\end{document}Sp[H,X] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]$$\end{document}Sq[H,X] in Lemma 3.1. By iteration, this will allow us to derive tractable formulas for the norms of multi-index Schatten norms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {S}_{p_1}[{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_{A_1},\mathcal {S}_{p_2}[...\,\mathcal {S}_{p_k}({{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_{A_k})\dots ]]$$\end{document}Sp1[HA1,Sp2[...Spk(HAk)⋯]]. In particular we derive variational formulas for systems made of three subsystems in Theorem 3.4.

Pisier’s formula Theorem 2.3 gives a way of relating the p-norm on the “left” most system to the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\infty $$\end{document}∞-norm on it. In the following, we refine it to relate the p-norm to its q-norm, as done in Theorem 2.5. Here, we keep \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X}$$\end{document}X general, so the following result generalizes Theorem 2.5, which can be recovered in the special case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X}=\mathcal {S}_q(\mathcal {K})$$\end{document}X=Sq(K). Although the proof closely follows that of the mentioned special case, we provide it here for completeness.

Lemma 3.1

(Generalized variational expressions). Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X}$$\end{document}X be an operator space. Then for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le p\le q\le \infty $$\end{document}1≤p≤q≤∞ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{r}=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}$$\end{document}1r=1p-1q, the following variational formulas hold for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X\in \mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}],\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]$$\end{document}X∈Sp[H,X],Sq[H,X] respectively:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \operatorname {(i)}\qquad&\Vert X\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]}=\inf _{\begin{array}{c} F,G\in \mathcal {S}_{2r}(\mathcal {H}), Y\in \mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H}, \mathcal {X}] \\ X=FYG \end{array}} \Vert F\Vert _{2r} \Vert Y\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_q[{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}},\mathcal {X}]}\Vert G\Vert _{2r}\\ \operatorname {(ii)}\qquad&\Vert X\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]} = \sup _{F,G\in \mathcal {S}_{2r}(\mathcal {H})}\Vert F\Vert _{2r}^{-1}\Vert FXG\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]}\Vert G\Vert _{2r}^{-1}\,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(i)‖X‖Sp[H,X]=infF,G∈S2r(H),Y∈Sq[H,X]X=FYG‖F‖2r‖Y‖Sq[H,X]‖G‖2r(ii)‖X‖Sq[H,X]=supF,G∈S2r(H)‖F‖2r-1‖FXG‖Sp[H,X]‖G‖2r-1.

The power of this theorem is that it can be iterated by choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}}$$\end{document}X itself to be composed of many subsystems with Schatten spaces with potentially different indices.

Proof

We will prove (i) by separately showing upper and lower bounds and (ii) will then follow by duality. Note that to keep notations short and as done before, we omit writing identity operators, e.g. for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F,G\in \mathcal {S}_{2r}(\mathcal {H})$$\end{document}F,G∈S2r(H) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y\in \mathcal {S}_\infty [\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]$$\end{document}Y∈S∞[H,X] we will write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FYG\equiv (F\otimes \mathbb {1})Y(G\otimes \mathbb {1})$$\end{document}FYG≡(F⊗1)Y(G⊗1).

(i) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X=FYG\in \mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]$$\end{document}X=FYG∈Sp[H,X] with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F,G\in \mathcal {S}_{2r}(\mathcal {H}),Y\in \mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H},X]$$\end{document}F,G∈S2r(H),Y∈Sq[H,X] and suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y=HZK$$\end{document}Y=HZK with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H,K\in \mathcal {S}_{2q}(\mathcal {H}), Z\in \mathcal {S}_\infty [\mathcal {H},{\mathcal {X}}]$$\end{document}H,K∈S2q(H),Z∈S∞[H,X], then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X=FH Z KG$$\end{document}X=FHZKG and by Hölder’s inequality \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert FH\Vert _{2p}\le \Vert F\Vert _{2r}\Vert H\Vert _{2q}$$\end{document}‖FH‖2p≤‖F‖2r‖H‖2q, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{q}$$\end{document}1p=1r+1q, we get \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FH,KG\in \mathcal {S}_{2p}(\mathcal {H})$$\end{document}FH,KG∈S2p(H). Hence\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert X\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]}&\le \Vert FH\Vert _{2p}\Vert Z\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_\infty [\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]}\Vert KG\Vert _{2p} \\&\le \Vert F\Vert _{2r}\Vert H\Vert _{2q}\Vert Z\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_\infty [\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]}\Vert K\Vert _{2q}\Vert G\Vert _{2r}\,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖X‖Sp[H,X]≤‖FH‖2p‖Z‖S∞[H,X]‖KG‖2p≤‖F‖2r‖H‖2q‖Z‖S∞[H,X]‖K‖2q‖G‖2r.After minimization over H, K, Z, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\Vert X\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]} \le \Vert F\Vert _{2r}\Vert Y\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]}\Vert G\Vert _{2r} \implies \Vert X\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]} \\&\quad \le \inf _{\begin{array}{c} F,G\in \mathcal {S}_{2r}\\ Y\in \mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}] \end{array}}\Vert F\Vert _{2r}\Vert Y\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]}\Vert G\Vert _{2r}\,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖X‖Sp[H,X]≤‖F‖2r‖Y‖Sq[H,X]‖G‖2r⇒‖X‖Sp[H,X]≤infF,G∈S2rY∈Sq[H,X]‖F‖2r‖Y‖Sq[H,X]‖G‖2r.On the other hand, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\epsilon >0$$\end{document}ϵ>0. Then there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y\in \mathcal {S}_\infty [\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]$$\end{document}Y∈S∞[H,X] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖X‖Sp[H,X]+ϵ≥‖F‖2p‖Y‖S∞[H,X]‖G‖2p.By performing a polar decomposition of F and G and absorbing the unitaries into Y, which does not change the norm, we may assume F, G to \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F^{\frac{p}{r}},G^{\frac{p}{r}}\in \mathcal {S}_{2r}(\mathcal {H})$$\end{document}Fpr,Gpr∈S2r(H). As a result, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X = F^{\frac{p}{r}} F^{\frac{p}{q}} Y G^{\frac{p}{q}} G^{\frac{p}{r}}$$\end{document}X=FprFpqYGpqGpr. Hence\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\inf _{\begin{array}{c} X=HWK\\ H,K\in \mathcal {S}_{2r}(\mathcal {H}), W\in \mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}], \end{array}}\Vert H\Vert _{2r}\Vert W\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]}\Vert K\Vert _{2r} \\&\quad \le \Vert F^{\frac{p}{r}}\Vert _{2r}\Vert F^{\frac{p}{q}}YG^{\frac{p}{q}}\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]}\Vert G^{\frac{p}{r}}\Vert _{2r} \\&\quad \le \Vert F^{\frac{p}{r}}\Vert _{2r}\Vert F^{\frac{p}{q}}\Vert _{2q}\Vert Y\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_\infty [\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]}\Vert G^{\frac{p}{q}}\Vert _{2q}\Vert G^{\frac{p}{r}}\Vert _{2r} \\&\quad =\Vert F\Vert _{2p}\Vert Y\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_\infty [\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]} \Vert G\Vert _{2p} \\&\quad = \Vert X\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]}+\epsilon . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}infX=HWKH,K∈S2r(H),W∈Sq[H,X],‖H‖2r‖W‖Sq[H,X]‖K‖2r≤‖Fpr‖2r‖FpqYGpq‖Sq[H,X]‖Gpr‖2r≤‖Fpr‖2r‖Fpq‖2q‖Y‖S∞[H,X]‖Gpq‖2q‖Gpr‖2r=‖F‖2p‖Y‖S∞[H,X]‖G‖2p=‖X‖Sp[H,X]+ϵ.Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\epsilon >0$$\end{document}ϵ>0 is arbitrary the claim follows.

(ii) by the duality \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {S}_{q}[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]^*=\mathcal {S}_{q^\prime }[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}^*]$$\end{document}Sq[H,X]∗=Sq′[H,X∗], i.e. there is a complete isometry between these two operator spaces, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1/q+1/q^\prime =1$$\end{document}1/q+1/q′=1 (see [6, Proposition 4.3]). For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1/p + 1/p^\prime = 1$$\end{document}1/p+1/p′=1, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 \le p^\prime \le q^\prime $$\end{document}1≤p′≤q′ and also \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{r} = \frac{1}{p^\prime } - \frac{1}{q^\prime }$$\end{document}1r=1p′-1q′, we thus obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert X\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]}&=\sup _Y\frac{|\hbox {Tr}[Y^*X]|}{\Vert Y\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_{q\prime }[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}^*]}}\\&\overset{\operatorname {(i)}}{=} \sup _Y\sup _{ Y = FZG}\frac{|\hbox {Tr}[Y^*X]|}{\Vert F\Vert _{2r}\Vert G\Vert _{2r}\Vert Z\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_{p^\prime }[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}^*]}} \\ &=\sup _{F,G,Z}\frac{|\hbox {Tr}[G^*Z^*F^*X]|}{\Vert F\Vert _{2r}\Vert G\Vert _{2r}\Vert Z\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_{p^\prime }[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}^*]}}\\&=\sup _{F,G,Z}\frac{|\hbox {Tr}[Z^*(F^*XG^*)]|}{\Vert F\Vert _{2r}\Vert G\Vert _{2r}\Vert Z\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_{p^\prime }[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}^*]}}\\&=\sup _{F,G}\frac{\Vert FXG\Vert _{\mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H},\mathcal {X}]}}{\Vert F\Vert _{2r}\Vert G\Vert _{2r}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖X‖Sq[H,X]=supY|Tr[Y∗X]|‖Y‖Sq′[H,X∗]=(i)supYsupY=FZG|Tr[Y∗X]|‖F‖2r‖G‖2r‖Z‖Sp′[H,X∗]=supF,G,Z|Tr[G∗Z∗F∗X]|‖F‖2r‖G‖2r‖Z‖Sp′[H,X∗]=supF,G,Z|Tr[Z∗(F∗XG∗)]|‖F‖2r‖G‖2r‖Z‖Sp′[H,X∗]=supF,G‖FXG‖Sp[H,X]‖F‖2r‖G‖2r.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Since for our applications we are mainly interested in operator-valued Schatten norms over 3 indices, we derive explicit variational formulas for the latter from the above lemma. However, in principle the same approach leads one to explicit variational formulas for arbitrarily many different indices. This is the main technical result of this section.

Theorem 3.2

(Variational formulas 1). Consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X_{123}\in \mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H}_1,\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H}_2,\mathcal {S}_s(\mathcal {H}_3)]]$$\end{document}X123∈Sp[H1,Sq[H2,Ss(H3)]]. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le p\le q\le s\le \infty $$\end{document}1≤p≤q≤s≤∞ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{r}:=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{s}$$\end{document}1r:=1p-1s and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \frac{1}{r^\prime }:=\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{s}$$\end{document}1r′:=1q-1s, it holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert X\Vert _{(1:p,2:q,3:s)} = \inf _{ X=G_{12}Y_{123}F_{12}}\Vert GG^*\Vert ^{\frac{1}{2}}_{(1:r,2:r^\prime )}\Vert F^*F\Vert ^{\frac{1}{2}}_{(1:r,2:r^\prime )}\Vert Y\Vert _s\,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖X‖(1:p,2:q,3:s)=infX=G12Y123F12‖GG∗‖(1:r,2:r′)12‖F∗F‖(1:r,2:r′)12‖Y‖s.For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le s\le q\le p\le \infty $$\end{document}1≤s≤q≤p≤∞ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{r}:=\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{p}$$\end{document}1r:=1s-1p and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{r^\prime }:=\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{q}$$\end{document}1r′:=1s-1q,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert X\Vert _{(1:p,2:q,3:s)} = \sup _{G_{12},F_{12}}\Vert G^*G\Vert ^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{(1:r,2:r^\prime )}\Vert FF^*\Vert ^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{(1:r,2:r^\prime )}\Vert G_{12}X_{123}F_{12}\Vert _s. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖X‖(1:p,2:q,3:s)=supG12,F12‖G∗G‖(1:r,2:r′)-12‖FF∗‖(1:r,2:r′)-12‖G12X123F12‖s.

These two expressions should be thought of as generalizations of Theorem 2.5 to three subsystems, for the above specified order of indices. A direct consequence of the Theorem is that the multi-index Piser norm of tensor product operators splits on the right, as compared to Proposition 2.7:

Corollary 3.3

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X\in \mathcal {S}_{p}[\mathcal {H}_1,\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H}_2,\mathcal {S}_s(\mathcal {H}_3)]]$$\end{document}X∈Sp[H1,Sq[H2,Ss(H3)]] s.t. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X=Y_{12}\otimes Z_3$$\end{document}X=Y12⊗Z3, then if either \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le p\le q\le s\le \infty ,$$\end{document}1≤p≤q≤s≤∞, or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le s\le q\le p\le \infty ,$$\end{document}1≤s≤q≤p≤∞, it holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert Y_{12}\otimes Z_3\Vert _{(1:p,2:q,3:s)} = \Vert Y\Vert _{(1:p,2:q)}\cdot \Vert Z\Vert _s \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Y12⊗Z3‖(1:p,2:q,3:s)=‖Y‖(1:p,2:q)·‖Z‖s

Proof

The proof follows from the multiplicativity of the Schatten-s-norm and the fact that relating the (p, q, s) to the (s, s, s) norm can be done by only affecting the first two systems. Depending on the order of p, q, s we apply the corresponding variational formula from Theorem 3.2. In the case of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le s\le q\le p\le \infty $$\end{document}1≤s≤q≤p≤∞ we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert Y_{12}\otimes Z_3\Vert _{(1:p,2:q,3:s)}&= \sup _{G_{12},F_{12}}\Vert G^*G\Vert ^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{(1:r,2:r^\prime )}\Vert FF^*\Vert ^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{(1:r,2:r^\prime )}\Vert G_{12}Y_{12}F_{12}\otimes Z_3\Vert _s \\ &= \sup _{G_{12},F_{12}}\Vert G^*G\Vert ^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{(1:r,2:r^\prime )}\Vert FF^*\Vert ^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{(1:r,2:r^\prime )}\Vert G_{12}Y_{12}F_{12}\Vert _s \cdot \Vert Z_3\Vert _s \\ &= \Vert Y\Vert _{1:p,2:q}\Vert Z\Vert _s. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Y12⊗Z3‖(1:p,2:q,3:s)=supG12,F12‖G∗G‖(1:r,2:r′)-12‖FF∗‖(1:r,2:r′)-12‖G12Y12F12⊗Z3‖s=supG12,F12‖G∗G‖(1:r,2:r′)-12‖FF∗‖(1:r,2:r′)-12‖G12Y12F12‖s·‖Z3‖s=‖Y‖1:p,2:q‖Z‖s.In the case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le p\le q\le s$$\end{document}1≤p≤q≤s we can by an argument as in Corollary 2.4 assume \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G,F\ge 0$$\end{document}G,F≥0 and apply the same as above now to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert G_{12}^{-1}Y_{12}F^{-1}_{12}\otimes Z_3\Vert _s=\Vert G_{12}^{-1}Y_{12}F^{-1}_{12}\Vert _{s}\cdot \Vert Z_3\Vert _s$$\end{document}‖G12-1Y12F12-1⊗Z3‖s=‖G12-1Y12F12-1‖s·‖Z3‖s. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

In a similar fashion to Theorem 3.2 we can also generalize Pisier’s formula and [10, Equation (3.3)]. We do this in the following.

Theorem 3.4

(Variational Formulas 2). Consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$X_{123}\in \mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H}_1,\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H}_2,\mathcal {X}]]$$\end{document}X123∈Sp[H1,Sq[H2,X]] for any operator space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X}$$\end{document}X, then for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le p,q\le \infty $$\end{document}1≤p,q≤∞ it holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert X\Vert _{(1:p,2:q;\mathcal {X})}\le \inf _{ X=G_{12}Z_{123}F_{12}}\Vert GG^*\Vert ^{\frac{1}{2}}_{(1:p,2:q)}\Vert F^*F\Vert ^{\frac{1}{2}}_{(1:p,2:q)}\Vert Z\Vert _{(1:\infty ,2:\infty ;\mathcal {X})}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖X‖(1:p,2:q;X)≤infX=G12Z123F12‖GG∗‖(1:p,2:q)12‖F∗F‖(1:p,2:q)12‖Z‖(1:∞,2:∞;X),where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(1:q,2:p;\mathcal {X})$$\end{document}(1:q,2:p;X) is a shorthand for the norm on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H}_1,\mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H}_2,\mathcal {X}]]$$\end{document}Sq[H1,Sp[H2,X]]. Further for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le p\le q\le \infty $$\end{document}1≤p≤q≤∞ equality holds.

Proof of Theorem 3.2

The proof consists of repeated applications of Lemma 3.1 combined with simplifications due to Theorem 2.5. The first formula follows from Lemma 3.1 (i), first with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X} = \mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H}_2,\mathcal {S}_s(\mathcal {H}_3)]$$\end{document}X=Sq[H2,Ss(H3)] and then with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X} = \mathcal {S}_s(\mathcal {H}_3)$$\end{document}X=Ss(H3):\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert X\Vert _{(1:p,2:q,3:s)}&= \inf _{X_{123} =F_1Y_{123}G_1} \Vert F\Vert _{2\alpha }\Vert G\Vert _{2\alpha }\Vert Y\Vert _{(1:q,2:q,3:s)} \nonumber \\&= \inf _{X_{123} =F_1Y_{123}G_1} \Vert F\Vert _{2\alpha }\Vert G\Vert _{2\alpha }\Vert Y\Vert _{(12:q,3:s)} \\ &= \inf _{ X_{123}=F_1 H_{12} Z_{123} K_{12} G_1} \Vert F\Vert _{2\alpha } \Vert G\Vert _{2\alpha } \Vert H\Vert _{2r^\prime } \Vert K\Vert _{2r^\prime } \Vert Z\Vert _s \\ &= \inf _{\begin{array}{c} X_{123}=F_1H_{12}Z_{123}K_{12}G_1\\ F_1, G_1\ge 0,H_{12},K_{12} \end{array}} \Vert F\Vert _{2\alpha } \Vert G\Vert _{2\alpha } \Vert HH^*\Vert ^{\frac{1}{2}}_{r^\prime } \Vert K^*K\Vert ^{\frac{1}{2}}_{r^\prime } \Vert Z\Vert _s \\ &= \inf _{\begin{array}{c} X_{123}=M_{12} Z_{123} N_{12}\\ F_1,G_1\ge 0 \end{array}} \Vert F\Vert _{2\alpha } \Vert G\Vert _{2\alpha } \Vert F_1^{-1}M_{12}M_{12}^*F_1^{-1}\Vert ^{\frac{1}{2}}_{r^\prime }\\&\quad \Vert G_1^{-1}N_{12}^*N_{12}G_1^{-1}\Vert ^{\frac{1}{2}}_{r^\prime } \Vert Z\Vert _s \\ &= \inf _{ X_{123}=M_{12}Z_{123}N_{12}}\Vert M_{12}M_{12}^*\Vert ^{\frac{1}{2}}_{(1:r,2:r^\prime )}\Vert N_{12}^*N_{12}\Vert ^{\frac{1}{2}}_{(1:r,2:r^\prime )}\Vert Z\Vert _s \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖X‖(1:p,2:q,3:s)=infX123=F1Y123G1‖F‖2α‖G‖2α‖Y‖(1:q,2:q,3:s)=infX123=F1Y123G1‖F‖2α‖G‖2α‖Y‖(12:q,3:s)=infX123=F1H12Z123K12G1‖F‖2α‖G‖2α‖H‖2r′‖K‖2r′‖Z‖s=infX123=F1H12Z123K12G1F1,G1≥0,H12,K12‖F‖2α‖G‖2α‖HH∗‖′12‖K∗K‖′12‖Z‖s=infX123=M12Z123N12F1,G1≥0‖F‖2α‖G‖2α‖F1-1M12M12∗F1-1‖′12‖G1-1N12∗N12G1-1‖′12‖Z‖s=infX123=M12Z123N12‖M12M12∗‖(1:r,2:r′)12‖N12∗N12‖(1:r,2:r′)12‖Z‖swhere in the fourth line we restricted to positive F, G by polar decomposition, absorbed unitaries into H, K respectively, and set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M\equiv FH$$\end{document}M≡FH, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N\equiv KG$$\end{document}N≡KG and thus \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H=F^{-1}M$$\end{document}H=F-1M, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K=NG^{-1}$$\end{document}K=NG-1, where these inverses respectively are the generalized Moore-Penrose inverses of F, G. There we also used that the Schatten norms of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^*H$$\end{document}H∗H and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$HH^*$$\end{document}HH∗ are equal, since their non-zero singular values, which are equal to their eigenvalues, are equal. In the last line, we used the expression of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \cdot \Vert _{(1:r,2:r^\prime )}$$\end{document}‖·‖(1:r,2:r′) given in Theorem 2.5 and the fact that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{\alpha }=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{r^\prime }=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}$$\end{document}1α=1r-1r′=1p-1q. We recall that since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_{12}M_{12}^*$$\end{document}M12M12∗ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N^*_{12}N_{12}$$\end{document}N12∗N12 are positive, their 2-indexed Schatten norms are can be written by optimizations over just one matrix-variable F, G, respectively.

The second formula follows analogously after applying Lemma 3.1 (ii). We define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{\alpha }=\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{p}$$\end{document}1α=1q-1p.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\Vert X\Vert _{(1:p,2:q,3:s)} \\&\qquad = \sup _{F_1,G_1} \Vert F\Vert ^{-1}_{2\alpha }\Vert G\Vert ^{-1}_{2\alpha }\Vert F_1 X_{123}G_1\Vert _{(1:q,2:q,3:s)} \\&\qquad =\sup _{\begin{array}{c} 0\le F_1,G_1\\ H_{12},K_{12} \end{array}}\Vert F\Vert ^{-1}_{2\alpha }\Vert G\Vert ^{-1}_{2\alpha }\Vert H\Vert ^{-1}_{2r^\prime }\Vert K\Vert ^{-1}_{2r^\prime } \Vert H_{12}F_1X_{123}G_1K_{12}\Vert _s \\&\qquad = \sup _{\begin{array}{c} 0\le F_1,G_1\\ M_{12},N_{12} \end{array}}\Vert F\Vert ^{-1}_{2\alpha }\Vert G\Vert ^{-1}_{2\alpha }\Vert F_1^{-1}M_{12}^*M_{12}F_1^{-1}\Vert ^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{2r^\prime }\Vert G_1^{-1}N_{12}N^*_{12}G_1^{-1}\Vert ^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{r^\prime } \Vert M_{12}X_{123}N_{12}\Vert _s \\&\qquad =\!\!\!\sup _{ M_{12},N_{12}}\!\!\!\left( \inf _{0<F_1}\!\!\!\Vert F\Vert ^2_{2\alpha }\Vert F_1^{-1}M_{12}^*M_{12}F_1^{-1}\Vert _{r^\prime }\!\!\right) ^{\!\!\!-\frac{1}{2}}\\&\quad \!\!\left( \inf _{0<G_1}\!\!\! Similarly we prove the other variational expression.

Having established these variational formulas, we now turn our attention to applying them to prove our main results about the multiplicativity of certain (completely bounded) mixed operator norms under tensor products, including in particular \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\rightarrow (1,p)$$\end{document}1→(1,p) norms.

Main Results: Chain Rules and Additivity

We now use the operator-valued Schatten- and Pisier-norms introduced in the previous sections to establish useful properties of entropic measures evaluated on a composite system. We start with a chain rule that allows decomposing the entropy of a joint system ST into the appropriate entropies of S and T.

Chain rules

Our chain rule will follow from the following multiplicativity statement stating that an identity “on the right” does not affect the norm of CP maps.

Theorem 4.1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi $$\end{document}Φ be a CP map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi :QP\rightarrow RS$$\end{document}Φ:QP→RS, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X}$$\end{document}X an operator space and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le q\le p\le \infty ,1\le r,s\le \infty $$\end{document}1≤q≤p≤∞,1≤r,s≤∞ then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \Phi \otimes {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_{\mathcal {X}}\Vert _{(Q:q,P:p;\mathcal {X})\rightarrow (R:r,S:s;\mathcal {X})} = \Vert \Phi \Vert ^+_{(Q:q,P:p)\rightarrow (R:r,S:s)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Φ⊗idX‖(Q:q,P:p;X)→(R:r,S:s;X)=‖Φ‖(Q:q,P:p)→(R:r,S:s)+,where the superscript \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$^+$$\end{document}+ denotes optimization over positive semidefinite operators.

This result is a generalization of [10, Lemma 5]. In fact, [10, Lemma 5] corresponds to the special case where P and S are trivial and our proof strategy closely follows the one in [10].

Before giving the proof of this result, we discuss some consequences. A first immediate one is that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \Phi \Vert _{(Q:q,P:p)\rightarrow (R:r,S:s)} = \Vert \Phi \Vert ^+_{(Q:q,P:p)\rightarrow (R:r,S:s)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Φ‖(Q:q,P:p)→(R:r,S:s)=‖Φ‖(Q:q,P:p)→(R:r,S:s)+,for the above specified indices. A second direct consequence are two chain rules expressed in terms of conditional Rényi entropies. The first one is

Corollary 4.2

(Amortized Chain rule). For any state \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho \in \mathcal {D}(\rho _{QPT})$$\end{document}ρ∈D(ρQPT) on system QPT and any quantum channel \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi :QP\rightarrow RS$$\end{document}Φ:QP→RS we have the following chain rule for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha > 1$$\end{document}α>1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} H_\alpha ^\uparrow (ST|R)_{(\Phi \otimes {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_T)(\rho )} - H_\alpha ^\uparrow (PT|Q)_{\rho } \ge \inf _{\omega \in {{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {D}}\,}}({{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_{QP})} \left( H_\alpha ^\uparrow (S|R)_{\Phi (\omega )}-H^\uparrow _\alpha (P|Q)_\omega \right) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hα↑(ST|R)(Φ⊗idT)(ρ)-Hα↑(PT|Q)ρ≥infω∈D(HQP)Hα↑(S|R)Φ(ω)-Hα↑(P|Q)ω

The second one is

Corollary 4.3

(Chain rule). For any state \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho \in \mathcal {D}({{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_{QT})$$\end{document}ρ∈D(HQT) on systems QT and any quantum channel \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi :Q\rightarrow RS$$\end{document}Φ:Q→RS, we have the chain rule for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha > 1$$\end{document}α>19\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&H^{\uparrow }_\alpha (ST|R)_{(\Phi \otimes {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_T)(\rho _{QT})} - H^{\uparrow }_\alpha (T|Q)_\rho \nonumber \\&\quad \ge \inf _{\sigma \in \mathcal {D}({{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_{Q})} H^{\uparrow }_\alpha (S|R)_{\Phi (\sigma _{Q})}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hα↑(ST|R)(Φ⊗idT)(ρQT)-Hα↑(T|Q)ρ≥infσ∈D(HQ)Hα↑(S|R)Φ(σQ),where the inequality is saturated for density operators \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _{QT}=\rho _{Q}\otimes \rho _T$$\end{document}ρQT=ρQ⊗ρT where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _Q$$\end{document}ρQ achieves the infimum on the right-hand side expression.

Remark 4.4

This second chain rule (9) should be compared to the one in [17, Lemma 3.6] with the replacements \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q \rightarrow E$$\end{document}Q→E, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\emptyset \rightarrow R$$\end{document}∅→R, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S \rightarrow A'$$\end{document}S→A′, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T \rightarrow A$$\end{document}T→A, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R \rightarrow E'$$\end{document}R→E′. Our chain rule is less general in the sense that the system R in [17, Lemma 3.6] is chosen to be trivial (this is the relevant setting for the analysis of prepare-and-measure protocols [18]), but it has some advantages/differences: firstly, it is directly for the optimized Rényi entropy, secondly, we obtain a slight improvement in that we do not need a purifying system on the right-hand side of (9), and thirdly, we have no loss in the parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α.

Proof of Corollary 4.2 and Corollary 4.3

They follow both directly from Theorem 4.1 by setting \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X}=S_\alpha (\mathcal {H}_T)$$\end{document}X=Sα(HT) and applying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{\alpha }{1-\alpha }\log $$\end{document}α1-αlog. The amortized one follows when setting \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q=r=1, p=s=\alpha > 1$$\end{document}q=r=1,p=s=α>1, whereas (9) by setting P trivial and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p=q=r=1, s=\alpha > 1$$\end{document}p=q=r=1,s=α>1. Then the left-hand sides become, respectively, with or without the P\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \inf _{\rho _{QPT}\ge 0}\left( H^{\uparrow }_\alpha (ST|R)_{(\Phi \otimes {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_T)(\rho _{QPT})} - H^{\uparrow }_\alpha (PT|Q)_{\rho _{QT}}\right) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}infρQPT≥0Hα↑(ST|R)(Φ⊗idT)(ρQPT)-Hα↑(PT|Q)ρQTand right-hand side,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \inf _{\sigma _{QP}\ge 0} \left( H_\alpha ^\uparrow (S|R)_{\Phi (\sigma )}-H^\uparrow _\alpha (P|Q)_\sigma \right) , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}infσQP≥0Hα↑(S|R)Φ(σ)-Hα↑(P|Q)σ,respectively,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \inf _{\sigma \in \mathcal {D}(\mathcal {H}_Q)} H^{\uparrow }_\alpha (S|R)_{\Phi (\sigma _{Q})}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}infσ∈D(HQ)Hα↑(S|R)Φ(σQ).Due to positive homogeneity the suprema over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma \ge 0$$\end{document}σ≥0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma \in {{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {D}}\,}}({{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_Q)$$\end{document}σ∈D(HQ) are equal. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We now proceed with the proof of Theorem 4.1, which as previously mentioned closely follows [10, Lemma 5] up to using our Theorem 3.4 in place of Theorem 2.3.

Proof of Theorem 4.1

First of all notice that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \Phi \otimes {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_{\mathcal {X}}\Vert _{(Q:q,P:p;\mathcal {X})\rightarrow (R:r,S:s;\mathcal {X})} \ge \Vert \Phi \Vert ^+_{(Q:q,P:p)\rightarrow (R:r,S:s)}$$\end{document}‖Φ⊗idX‖(Q:q,P:p;X)→(R:r,S:s;X)≥‖Φ‖(Q:q,P:p)→(R:r,S:s)+, since we can just restrict the supremum on the left hand side over product operators. In the following we will extend this to completely bounded norms in the following way.

Corollary 4.5

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi :Q \rightarrow RS$$\end{document}Φ:Q→RS be a CP map, then for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le q,p\le \infty $$\end{document}1≤q,p≤∞\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \Phi \otimes {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_{{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}}}\Vert _{cb,(Q:q,{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}})\rightarrow (R:q,S:p,{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}})}= \Vert \Phi \Vert ^+_{cb,Q:q\rightarrow (R:q,S:p)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Φ⊗idX‖cb,(Q:q,X)→(R:q,S:p,X)=‖Φ‖cb,Q:q→(R:q,S:p)+.

Proof

We have, using the above Theorem 4.1 that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\Vert \Phi \otimes {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_{{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}}}\Vert _{cb,(Q:q;{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}})\rightarrow (R:q,S:p;{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}})} = \sup _E\Vert \underbrace{{{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_E\otimes \Phi }_{=:\Psi _E}\otimes {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_{{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}}}\Vert _{(E:q,Q:q;{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}})\rightarrow (E:q,R:q,S:p;{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}})}\\&\quad \equiv \sup _E\Vert \Psi _E\otimes {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_{{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}}}\Vert _{(EQ:q;{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}})\rightarrow (ER:q,S:p;{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}})} \\&\quad {=} \sup _E\Vert \Psi _E\Vert ^+_{(EQ:q)\rightarrow (ER:q,S:p)}=\sup _E\Vert {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_E\otimes \Phi \Vert ^+_{(E:q,Q:q)\rightarrow (E:q,R:q,S:p)}\\&\quad = \Vert \Phi \Vert ^+_{cb,(Q:q)\rightarrow (R:q,S:p)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Φ⊗idX‖cb,(Q:q;X)→(R:q,S:p;X)=supE‖idE⊗Φ⏟=:ΨE⊗idX‖(E:q,Q:q;X)→(E:q,R:q,S:p;X)≡supE‖ΨE⊗idX‖(EQ:q;X)→(ER:q,S:p;X)=supE‖ΨE‖(EQ:q)→(ER:q,S:p)+=supE‖idE⊗Φ‖(E:q,Q:q)→(E:q,R:q,S:p)+=‖Φ‖cb,(Q:q)→(R:q,S:p)+.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

This result will be important in proving Theorem 4.11. It also has an interpretation as a chain rule for Rényi-entropies when fixing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q=1$$\end{document}q=1. Like above applying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{\alpha }{1-\alpha }\log $$\end{document}α1-αlog to the above directly yields

Corollary 4.6

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi :Q\rightarrow RS$$\end{document}Φ:Q→RS be a CP map For any system E and any state \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho \in \mathcal {D}({{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_{E}\otimes {{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_{Q} \otimes {{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_{T})$$\end{document}ρ∈D(HE⊗HQ⊗HT), we have the following chain rule\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&H^{\uparrow }_\alpha (ST|RE)_{({{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_E\otimes \Phi \otimes {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_T)(\rho _{EQT})} - H^{\uparrow }_\alpha (T|QE)_\rho \\&\quad \ge \inf _E\inf _{\sigma \in \mathcal {D}({{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_{QE})} H^{\uparrow }_\alpha (S|RE)_{({{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_E\otimes \Phi )(\sigma _{EQ})}\,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hα↑(ST|RE)(idE⊗Φ⊗idT)(ρEQT)-Hα↑(T|QE)ρ≥infEinfσ∈D(HQE)Hα↑(S|RE)(idE⊗Φ)(σEQ).

Additivity

Making use of the previously established technical Lemmas, we present a general multiplicativity result for CB norms:

Theorem 4.7

(Multiplicativity of ordered CB norms). Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {X}}\,}}, {{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {Y}}\,}}$$\end{document}X,Y be operator spaces and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 \le p,q \le \infty $$\end{document}1≤p,q≤∞. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi :{{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {S}}\,}}_q(\mathcal {H}_Q)\rightarrow {{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {S}}\,}}_p(\mathcal {H}_P),\Psi :\mathcal {X}\rightarrow \mathcal {Y}$$\end{document}Φ:Sq(HQ)→Sp(HP),Ψ:X→Y be CP maps, then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \Phi \otimes \Psi \Vert _{cb,S_q[\mathcal {H}_Q,\mathcal {X}]\rightarrow S_p[\mathcal {H}_P,\mathcal {Y}]} = \Vert \Phi \Vert _{cb,(Q:q)\rightarrow (P:p)}\Vert \Psi \Vert _{cb,\mathcal {X}\rightarrow \mathcal {Y}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Φ⊗Ψ‖cb,Sq[HQ,X]→Sp[HP,Y]=‖Φ‖cb,(Q:q)→(P:p)‖Ψ‖cb,X→Y.And as a direct consequence, it holds for CP maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{\Phi _i:Q_i\rightarrow P_i\}$$\end{document}{Φi:Qi→Pi} and numbers \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le q_i,p_i\le \infty $$\end{document}1≤qi,pi≤∞ that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \bigotimes _{i=1}^n\Phi _i\Vert _{cb,(Q_1:q_1,...,Q_n:q_n)\rightarrow (P_1:p_1,...,P_n:p_n)} = \prod _{i=1}^n\Vert \Phi _i\Vert ^+_{cb,(Q_i:q_i)\rightarrow (P_i:p_i)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖⨂i=1nΦi‖cb,(Q1:q1,...,Qn:qn)→(P1:p1,...,Pn:pn)=∏i=1n‖Φi‖cb,(Qi:qi)→(Pi:pi)+.

Remark 4.8

This result is a generalization of [10, Theorem 11 and Theorem 13 c)] that corresponds to the case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q=q_1 = \cdots = q_n$$\end{document}q=q1=⋯=qn and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p=p_1 = \cdots = p_n$$\end{document}p=p1=⋯=pn. Note that such multiplicativity statements under tensor products do not hold in general for non-CB norm, see e.g., [10, Section 5].

Proof of Theorem 4.7

This proof of the upper bound follows from a combination of Lemma 2.8 and Theorem 4.1. We apply the former to the maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Phi \otimes {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_{\mathcal {Y}}:\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H}_Q,\mathcal {Y}]\rightarrow \mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H_P,\mathcal {Y}}]$$\end{document}Φ⊗idY:Sq[HQ,Y]→Sp[HP,Y] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_Q\otimes \Psi ):\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H}_Q,\mathcal {X}]\rightarrow \mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H}_Q,\mathcal {Y}]$$\end{document}(idQ⊗Ψ):Sq[HQ,X]→Sq[HQ,Y] to get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\Vert \Phi \otimes \Psi \Vert _{cb,S_q[\mathcal {H}_Q,\mathcal {S}_p[\mathcal {H_P,\mathcal {Y}}]} \cdot \Vert {{\,\textrm{id}\,}}_Q\otimes \Psi \Vert _{cb,\mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H}_Q,\mathcal {X}]\rightarrow \mathcal {S}_q[\mathcal {H_Q,\mathcal {Y}}]}
