Then it is possible to achieve an asymptotic key rate given by the conditional von Neumann entropy, minimized over all possible states that reproduce the statistics \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_X^{\textrm{hon}}$$\end{document}qXhon: 16a\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} h(\mathcal {M},\mathcal {N},\tau ,q^{\textrm{hon}}_X) =&\inf _{E,\rho _{QE} \in {{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {D}}\,}}({{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_{QE})}&H(A|XE)_{\mathcal {M}(\rho _{QE})} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}h(M,N,τ,qXhon)=infE,ρQE∈D(HQE)H(A|XE)M(ρQE)16b\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\mathrm {subject\ to}&\mathcal {N}(\rho _{Q}) = \tau&\end{aligned}$$\end{document}subjecttoN(ρQ)=τ16c\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\hbox {tr}_A \circ \mathcal {M}(\rho _Q) = q_X^{\textrm{hon}}\,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}trA∘M(ρQ)=qXhon.

In other words, for every n there exists a function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g_n:\mathbb {X}^n\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}gn:Xn→R, such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \max _{\rho _{Q^nE}} \epsilon (\mathcal {M}^n,g_n,\rho _{Q^nE})&= 0\\ \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \textrm{rate}(\mathcal {M}^n, g_n, (q^{\textrm{hon}}_{X})^{\otimes n})&= h(\mathcal {M},\mathcal {N},\tau ,q^{\textrm{hon}}_X). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limn→∞maxρQnEϵ(Mn,gn,ρQnE)=0limn→∞rate(Mn,gn,(qXhon)⊗n)=h(M,N,τ,qXhon).Time-dependent protocols

We generalize this to the case of protocols that vary in time, where we show that it is possible to achieve an asymptotic rate given by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} r_{ad} := \lim _{n \rightarrow \infty } \frac{1}{n} \sum _{t=1}^n h(\mathcal {M}_t,\mathcal {N}_t,\tau _t,q_{X_t}^{\textrm{hon}}) \,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}rad:=limn→∞1n∑t=1nh(Mt,Nt,τt,qXthon).To understand the advantage, we should compare this secret key rate with the one we obtain by applying a static security proof. First, note that when the protocol and the noise are static, there is no advantage in using a time-adaptive security proof. When both vary with time, the comparison cannot be made because static methods do not apply. However, we can easily make the comparison when the protocol is static, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}_t = \mathcal {M}$$\end{document}Mt=M, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _t = \tau $$\end{document}τt=τ, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}_t = \mathcal {N}$$\end{document}Nt=N, but the noise is not, i.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_{X_t}$$\end{document}qXt varies with t.

In this case, standard proof techniques [17, 23, 25] allow one to attain the secret key rate that corresponds to the average noise distribution \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{q}^{\textrm{hon}}_X = \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty }\frac{1}{n} \sum _{t=1}^n q^{\textrm{hon}}_{X_t}$$\end{document}q¯Xhon=limn→∞1n∑t=1nqthon. In other words, they allow us to construct \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g_n$$\end{document}gn such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \textrm{rate}(\mathcal {P}, g_n,q_{X^n}) = h(\mathcal {M},\mathcal {N},\tau , \overline{q}^{\textrm{hon}}_X) =: r_{na}\,, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limn→∞rate(P,gn,qXn)=h(M,N,τ,q¯Xhon)=:rna,However, the key rate obtained using our time-adaptive method is higher. This is because the function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h(\mathcal {M}, {{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {N}}\,}}, \tau , q_X)$$\end{document}h(M,N,τ,qX) is a convex function in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_X$$\end{document}qX in general [28] and strictly convex in most cases. In the latter case, there exists distribution \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_{X_t}^{\textrm{hon}}$$\end{document}qXthon such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} r_{na} = h(\mathcal {M},\mathcal {N},\tau , \overline{q}^{\textrm{hon}}_X) < \frac{1}{n} \sum _{t=1}^n h(\mathcal {M},\mathcal {N},\tau ,q^{\textrm{hon}}_{X_t}) = r_{ad}\,, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}rna=h(M,N,τ,q¯Xhon)<1n∑t=1nh(M,N,τ,qthon)=rad,which shows that the asymptotic key rate is higher using adaptive methods.

Security proof

We show that we can achieve the adaptive rate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{ad}$$\end{document}rad, under a technical assumptions on the honest distribution.

Theorem 5.2

(Time-adaptive protocol). Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}^n$$\end{document}Mn be a family of protocols as defined in Sect. 5.2 and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho ^{\textrm{hon}}_{Q_t} \in {{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {D}}\,}}({{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_{Q_t})$$\end{document}ρthon∈D(HQt) for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}t∈N be a family of quantum states such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{(\mathcal {M}_t,\mathcal {N}_t,\mathcal {\tau }_i,q^{\textrm{hon}}_{X_t})|t\in \mathbb {N}\}$$\end{document}{(Mt,Nt,τi,qthon)|t∈N} is a finite set and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho ^{\textrm{hon}}_{Q_t}> 0$$\end{document}ρthon>0 for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}t∈N. Then there exists a family of functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g_n: \mathbb {X}^n\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}gn:Xn→R which, for all n, lead to an \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\epsilon _n$$\end{document}ϵn-secure protocol, so that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \epsilon _n = 0$$\end{document}limn→∞ϵn=0 and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{n\rightarrow \infty } \textrm{rate}(\mathcal {P}_n, g_n,q^{\textrm{hon}}_{X^n}) = \lim _{n \rightarrow \infty } \frac{1}{n} \sum _{t=1}^n h(\mathcal {M}_t,\mathcal {N}_t,\tau _t,q_{X_t}^{\textrm{hon}})= r_{ad}\,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limn→∞rate(Pn,gn,qnhon)=limn→∞1n∑t=1nh(Mt,Nt,τt,qXthon)=rad.

Proof of Theorem 5.2

We first explain how to construct the functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g_n$$\end{document}gn. It is a standard results in QKD that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ h(\mathcal {M},\mathcal {N},\tau ,q_{X})$$\end{document}h(M,N,τ,qX) is a convex function in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_X$$\end{document}qX (see for example [28]). For each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}t∈N, the assumption \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho ^{\textrm{hon}}_{Q_t} > 0$$\end{document}ρthon>0 ensures that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_{X_t}^{\textrm{hon}}$$\end{document}qXthon is a strictly feasible distribution (i.e. the inequality constraints in the convex optimization problem (16) can be satisfied with strict inequalities). Consequently, there exists a supporting hyperplane to the graph of the function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_X \mapsto h(\mathcal {M}_t,\mathcal {N}_t,\tau _t, q_X)$$\end{document}qX↦h(Mt,Nt,τt,qX) at the point \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(q^{\textrm{hon}}_{X_t},h(\mathcal {M}_t,\mathcal {N}_t,\tau _t, q^{\textrm{hon}}_{X_t}))$$\end{document}(qthon,h(Mt,Nt,τt,qthon)). Moreover, we can chose the hyperplanes to be the same for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}t∈N such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\mathcal {M}_t,\mathcal {N}_t,\mathcal {\tau }_t,q^{\textrm{hon}}_{X_t})$$\end{document}(Mt,Nt,τt,qthon) are the same.

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_t:\mathbb {X}\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}ft:X→R be a function parametrizing the hyperplanes, so that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _x f_t(x) q_{X}(x) \le h(\mathcal {M}_t,\mathcal {N}_t,\tau _t,q_{X})$$\end{document}∑xft(x)qX(x)≤h(Mt,Nt,τt,qX) for all probability distributions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_X$$\end{document}qX, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _x f_t(x) q^{\textrm{hon}}_{X_t}(x) =h(\mathcal {M},\mathcal {N},$$\end{document}∑xft(x)qthon(x)=h(M,N, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau ,q^{\textrm{hon}}_{X_t})$$\end{document}τ,qthon). Note that a supporting hyperplane is given by an affine function, but the constant term can always be absorbed in the coefficients \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_t(x)$$\end{document}ft(x) since we require the inequality to hold for normalized probability distributions satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{x} q_{X}(x) = 1$$\end{document}∑xqX(x)=1. Recalling the definition of h, this guarantees that for every t and every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _{EXA} = \mathcal {M}_t(\rho _{EQ})$$\end{document}ρEXA=Mt(ρEQ) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _{EQ}\in {{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {D}}\,}}_r^E({{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_Q)$$\end{document}ρEQ∈DrE(HQ),\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} H(A|XE)_{\rho } \ge \mathbb {E}_{x \sim \rho _X}[f_t(x)]\,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}H(A|XE)ρ≥Ex∼ρX[ft(x)].We define our post-processing function by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g_n(x^n) = \max (0, \lfloor f^n(x^n) - \delta (\epsilon ,n)\rfloor )$$\end{document}gn(xn)=max(0,⌊fn(xn)-δ(ϵ,n)⌋) where\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} f^n(x^n) = \sum _{t=1}^n f_t(x_t)\,, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}fn(xn)=∑t=1nft(xt),and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta (n,\epsilon )$$\end{document}δ(n,ϵ) will be defined below.

We now have to show that this construction \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g_n$$\end{document}gn gives a correct lower-bound on the number of bits of randomness that can be extracted from \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A^n$$\end{document}An. Using the uniform continuity of f-weighted Rényi entropies (Lemma C.1), we find that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t \in [n]$$\end{document}t∈[n] and all states of the form \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _{EXA} = {{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {M}}\,}}_t(\rho _{QE})$$\end{document}ρEXA=Mt(ρQE) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _{EQ} \in {{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {D}}\,}}_r^E({{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_{Q})$$\end{document}ρEQ∈DrE(HQ)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} H_\alpha ^{\uparrow ,f_t}(A|XE)_{\rho } \ge -(\alpha -1) (\log \eta _t)^2 \,, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hα↑,ft(A|XE)ρ≥-(α-1)(logηt)2,for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in (1,1+1/\log \eta _t)$$\end{document}α∈(1,1+1/logηt) where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta _t$$\end{document}ηt only depends on the dimension of A and on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\max _x f_t(x)$$\end{document}maxxft(x) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\min _x f_t(x)$$\end{document}minxft(x). Moreover, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta _t$$\end{document}ηt takes only a finite set of values, we can bound \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta _t \le \eta = \max _{t\in \mathbb {N}} \eta _t$$\end{document}ηt≤η=maxt∈Nηt.

Using Corollary 5.1, this implies that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} H_\alpha ^{\uparrow , f_n}(A^n|X^nE)_{\rho } \ge - n (\alpha -1) (\log \eta )^2\,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hα↑,fn(An|XnE)ρ≥-n(α-1)(logη)2.for all state of the form \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _{EX^nA^n} = \mathcal {M}^n(\rho _{EQ^n})$$\end{document}ρEXnAn=Mn(ρEQn) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _{EQ^n}\in {{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {D}}\,}}_r^E({{\,\mathrm{\mathcal {H}}\,}}_{Q^n})$$\end{document}ρEQn∈DrE(HQn). Finally, using [25, Theorem 1], this implies that the choice \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta (n,\epsilon ) = n (\alpha -1) (\log \eta )^2 - \frac{\alpha }{\alpha -1}\log {1/\epsilon }$$\end{document}δ(n,ϵ)=n(α-1)(logη)2-αα-1log1/ϵ leads to an \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\epsilon $$\end{document}ϵ-secure protocol.

We now choose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha = 1 + \tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n}}$$\end{document}α=1+1n and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\epsilon _n = \frac{1}{n}$$\end{document}ϵn=1n. Then, the average rate given by our construction is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{n} \mathbb {E}_{x^n \sim \otimes _{t=1}^n q_{X_t}^{\textrm{hon}}}[g(x^n)]&\ge \frac{1}{n} \sum _{t=1}^n \sum _{x} f_t(x)q^{\textrm{hon}}_{X_t}(x) - \frac{(\log \eta )^2}{\sqrt{n}} - \frac{2}{\sqrt{n}} \log {1/\epsilon _n} -O(1)\\&= \frac{1}{n} \sum _{t=1}^n h(\mathcal {M}_t,\mathcal {N}_t,\tau _t,q^{\textrm{hon}}_{X_t}) - O(\tfrac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}1nExn∼⊗t=1nqXthon[g(xn)]≥1n∑t=1n∑xft(x)qthon(x)-(logη)2n-2nlog1/ϵn-O(1)=1n∑t=1nh(Mt,Nt,τt,qthon)-O(lognn)which yields the stated asymptotic limit. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Application to the BB84 protocol

For the BB84 protocol, it is known that in static cases the asymptotic key rate is given by the Shor-Preskill formula \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1-2h(p)$$\end{document}1-2h(p) where p is error on the channel, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h(p) = -p \log _2 p - (1-p)\log _2 (1-p)$$\end{document}h(p)=-plog2p-(1-p)log2(1-p) is the binary entropy. Using a standard source replacement scheme, the protocol can be represented in its equivalent entanglement-based representation. In this case, the input space to a round of the protocol is the joint qubit space of Alice and Bob \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q = Q_AQ_B$$\end{document}Q=QAQB with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}_{Q_A} = \mathcal {H}_{Q_B} = \mathbb {C}^{2}$$\end{document}HQA=HQB=C2.

Let us specify what \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M},\mathcal {N},\tau $$\end{document}M,N,τ are in this case. The map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}$$\end{document}M corresponds to the following physical process: Alice and Bob each generate a uniformly random basis choice, measure in the corresponding X or Z basis and announce their basis publicly. Alice then randomly decides if the round is a test round, with some fixed probability \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p_{\textrm{test}}$$\end{document}ptest, and announces this publicly. In this case, Alice and Bob announce their measurement results publicly and Alice sets \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A = \perp $$\end{document}A=⊥; otherwise Alice assigns her measurement results to A. The variable X regroups all public announcements that were made.

Because of source replacement scheme, the state \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho _{Q}$$\end{document}ρQ that is input in a round of the protocol must satisfy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hbox {tr}_{Q_B}[\rho _{Q}] = \frac{\mathbb {1}}{2}$$\end{document}trQB[ρQ]=12. Therefore we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q' = Q_A$$\end{document}Q′=QA, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N} = \hbox {tr}_{Q_B}$$\end{document}N=trQB and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau = \frac{\mathbb {1}}{2}$$\end{document}τ=12.

We consider a family of honest implementations of the form \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rho ^{\textrm{hon}}_{Q} = (1-2p)\phi ^+ + \frac{p}{2} \mathbb {1}_Q$$\end{document}ρQhon=(1-2p)ϕ++p21Q, with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi ^+$$\end{document}ϕ+ the maximally entangled state and p a parameter that varies with time, to be defined later. This corresponds to an error in the X and Z basis of probability \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Pr [X_A\ne X_B] = \Pr [Z_A \ne Z_B] = p$$\end{document}Pr[XA≠XB]=Pr[ZA≠ZB]=p. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_{X}^{\textrm{hon}}$$\end{document}qXhon be the corresponding distribution over public announcements. A standard result is that the asymptotic rate of randomness generation of the BB84 protocol is given by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} h(\mathcal {M},\mathcal {N},\tau ,q_{X}^{\textrm{hon}}) = 1 - h(p) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}h(M,N,τ,qXhon)=1-h(p)expressed in bits per key generation round.

Consider an honest distribution where p varies in times, so that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p=p_1=0.001$$\end{document}p=p1=0.001 for one third of the rounds and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p=p_2=0.1$$\end{document}p=p2=0.1 for the other two thirds, so that the average distribution corresponds to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{p} = \frac{1}{3} p_1 + \frac{2}{3} p_2\approx 0.067$$\end{document}p¯=13p1+23p2≈0.067. Computing the corresponding time-adaptive and non-adaptive randomness generation rates and subtracting the cost of error correction given by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$h(\bar{p})$$\end{document}h(p¯), we find that the asymptotic secure key generation rates SKrate are\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} SKrate&= \frac{1}{3}(1- h(p_1)) + \frac{2}{3} (1-h(p_2)) - h(\bar{p}) \approx 0.329&\qquad \text {(time-adaptive)}\\ SKrate&= 1- 2 h(\bar{p}) \approx 0.291&\qquad \text {(not-adaptive)} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}SKrate=13(1-h(p1))+23(1-h(p2))-h(p¯)≈0.329(time-adaptive)SKrate=1-2h(p¯)≈0.291(not-adaptive)This corresponds to an increase of 13% for time-adaptive methods compared to non adaptive methods.
